There are times where I’ll realize it’s late in the day and I haven’t really eaten anything, not because I’m trying not to, just because I forget or nothing sounds good. And when I do make myself eat, food kind of tastes like cardboard sometimes. Like I know I’m chewing something I usually like, but it just feels… pointless, I guess. So yeah, I think I’ve lost some weight, even if I don’t know exactly how much.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things that you think you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. A lot. It’s kind of embarrassing how much, because some of it is small stuff that I know other people probably wouldn’t even remember, but I keep turning it over in my head like it means something terrible about me.\n\nLike at work, if I make any mistake at the register — even something like counting change wrong for a second, or needing to call my manager over, or taking too long with a customer — I’ll replay it later over and over. I’ll be lying there thinking, why did I do that, why can’t I just be normal and do my job right, everyone must think I’m useless. And it doesn’t just feel like, “Oh, I made a mistake.” It feels like proof that I’m no good, like I’m letting my manager down and making things harder for everyone.\n\nAnd then it spreads to my family too. I keep thinking I’m disappointing them, like I’m not showing up the way I should. My little nieces especially… I feel awful because I think I should be more present, more fun, more like myself around them, and instead I feel like I’m just this person who’s failing everybody. Even if no one says that to me, my mind keeps saying it. Like I’ve let everybody down somehow, even when I can’t point to one huge thing I did.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing this heaviness and slowness consistently over the past four months, or has it varied?\n\nPatient: It’s varied, definitely. It hasn’t been the same the whole time. Earlier on, I was almost too sped up in my body, if that makes sense. Like at work I couldn’t just stand at the register and wait — I’d be pacing around the store, straightening things that were already straight, moving candy around, fixing shelves, wiping the counter again. It felt like I had this pressure in me and if I stopped moving I’d feel worse.\n\nBut lately it’s been more the opposite. The heaviness and slowness is more recent, or at least it’s what I notice most now. Even simple stuff feels like it takes effort, like walking to the bus feels way harder than it should, and when someone asks me a question, I know what they’re saying, but my answer comes out slow. Like there’s a pause before I can get the words moving.\n\nSo no, not consistently the same for the whole four months. It kind of shifted from restless and pacing to this slowed-down, heavy feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, such as hearing voices or seeing things that others can't?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve had that happen, and I don’t really like saying it out loud because it sounds, I don’t know, it sounds like I’m making it up or being dramatic, but it felt real when it was happening.\n\nIt’s mostly been at work, at the register. I’ll be standing there scanning things or waiting for someone’s card to go through, and I’ll hear what sounds like people right behind me whispering. Like close enough that I think a customer is standing too close, or someone is trying to get my attention without fully talking to me. A couple times it sounded like my name, like someone whispered it just behind my shoulder, and I’d turn around expecting somebody to be there, but there wouldn’t be anyone.\n\nAnd sometimes it wasn’t just my name. It would be like… little comments, like I was messing everything up, or doing it wrong, or that people were noticing. Not always loud, more like whispering, like customers talking under their breath but aimed at me. And I’d look around and everyone would just be doing normal stuff, or there wouldn’t even be anyone close enough to have said it.\n\nI kept trying to tell myself maybe it was the store noise, or the speakers, or someone in another aisle, because it’s embarrassing and scary to think it’s not that. But there were times where it really felt like someone was right behind me saying it, and then I’d turn and nobody was there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any thoughts that you think are being inserted into your mind or that you're being controlled by an outside force?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that exact way. Like, I’ve had thoughts that feel intrusive or like they come out of nowhere, especially when I’m already stressed, but I don’t know that I’ve felt like someone else was putting them there.\n\nAnd I don’t think I’ve felt controlled by something outside me, like someone moving me around or making me do things. I mean, sometimes I feel overwhelmed by my own mind, like I can’t get it to quiet down, but that still feels like it’s coming from me, if that makes sense. Not like a person or a force is controlling me.\n\nI’m not totally sure what would count, though. Like if you mean literally feeling like my thoughts aren’t mine, then no, I don’t think I’ve noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past week where you felt somewhat better, or has the heaviness and sadness been constant?\n\nPatient: It’s been pretty constant this past week, honestly. I mean, there might be tiny moments where I’m distracted or I’m not actively thinking about how bad I feel, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had a day where I felt better in any real way.\n\nThe tiredness is the biggest thing. That’s really why I came in, because I’m so tired I can barely get through a shift. It’s not like normal tired where you sleep or sit down for a while and then you get some energy back. It feels like my whole body is filled with wet sand, like every movement takes more effort than it should. Even when I rest, I don’t feel restored. I can lie down, or sit on the couch doing nothing, and when I get back up it’s like I never rested at all.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve had a good day this week. Maybe a few better minutes here and there, but the heaviness has mostly just stayed there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been able to enjoy anything at all recently, even if it's just for a moment?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I keep trying to, because I know I’m supposed to have things that make me feel like myself, but they just don’t land the same.\n\nLike cooking, for example. I usually love cooking for myself. Even if it’s something simple, I used to like picking what I wanted, chopping things, making it taste right, feeling like I did one nice thing for myself. Lately it just feels like a chore, like I’m moving food around because I have to eat, not because I care.\n\nAnd guitar too… I’ll pick it up sometimes because I think maybe it’ll help, and then I just sit there holding it. I might strum a little, but it feels flat, like there’s no feeling behind it. Same with hiking. I used to actually look forward to being outside and getting away from everything, but now even the thought of getting dressed and going somewhere feels pointless.\n\nEven going to my family’s place, which usually helps at least a little, feels like I’m kind of pretending. I’ll sit there and try to act normal and respond the right way, but inside I’m not really enjoying it. It’s like I remember that I’m supposed to care about these things, but I can’t actually feel it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you lost touch with reality?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think maybe I have, or at least I’ve had moments where I couldn’t trust what was happening around me.\n\nThe main thing was at work, at the register. I’d be ringing people up and I would hear what sounded like customers standing right behind me, whispering my name. Or saying things like I was messing everything up. Not loud like someone yelling, more like that low talking people do when they don’t want you to hear them but you still can. And it felt so real in the moment that I’d turn around expecting someone to be there.\n\nBut then nobody would be there. Or the people nearby would just be shopping or looking at their phones, not even paying attention to me. And then I’d feel embarrassed, because I’m standing there looking around like I heard something, but there’s nothing there.\n\nIt happened more than once, and it really scared me, because it wasn’t just me worrying someone was judging me. It sounded like an actual voice right behind me. I don’t know if that counts as losing touch with reality, but it definitely felt like my brain was telling me something was happening that wasn’t actually happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any visual hallucinations, such as seeing things that others cannot?\n\nPatient: Seeing things… not really, not like full-on seeing a person or something standing there that nobody else can see.\n\nFor me it’s been more hearing things. Like at the register, I would be working and I’d suddenly hear what sounded like customers right behind me whispering my name, or saying I was messing everything up. It was close enough and clear enough that I’d turn around because I really thought someone was there talking about me. But then there wouldn’t be anyone there, or nobody would be looking at me or acting like they said anything.\n\nSo visually, I don’t think so. It’s more that I hear it and then I look because I expect to see someone, and then there’s nothing there.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. No… I don’t think so. Not a whole day, at least.

It’s more like this low, empty feeling has just been there all the time. Maybe it shifts a little, like some hours are less heavy than others, but I can’t think of a day in the past two weeks where I woke up and felt actually okay, like no sadness or depression at all.

I don’t really like saying that out loud, because it sounds so dramatic, and part of me keeps trying to tell myself I’m just exhausted and having a bad moment, but… yes.\n\nI’ve had these thoughts where I’ll be lying there or getting ready for work and I think, maybe it would just be easier if I didn’t wake up. Or like… maybe everyone would be better off if I just wasn’t here taking up space and making things harder. I don’t want to scare anyone, and I feel guilty even saying it, because I know people care about me, but when I’m in that headspace it feels very convincing.\n\nAnd a few times on my way home, when I’m walking near the road, I’ve caught myself thinking about just stepping off the curb into traffic. Not like I’ve stood there planning it all out for hours, but the thought pops in, and it scares me afterward. Like I’ll realize what I just thought and I feel this cold feeling in my stomach, because it doesn’t feel like me, but it is my thought. So… yes, I’ve been having thoughts like that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about four months now, I think. Not just a bad week or two. I keep trying to look back and figure out exactly when it started, because at first I thought maybe I was just coming down from this period where I had a lot of energy at work, like I was pushing really hard and running on adrenaline or something.\n\nBut that passed, and then this low, empty feeling just… stayed. It didn’t lift the way I expected it to. So yeah, around four months of feeling like this, where everything feels heavy and flat and I’m not really myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite, feeling restless and fidgety?\n\nPatient: Yeah… both, actually, but at different times. At first I was almost the opposite of slowed down. I couldn’t stop moving. Like at the store, I’d be pacing around even when there wasn’t really anything urgent to do, straightening the candy shelves, fixing the bags, lining up receipts, wiping the counter again even if I’d already done it. It wasn’t even productive, exactly. It was more like if I stood still, I felt like I was going to crawl out of my skin.\n\nBut lately it’s changed. Now I feel… heavy. Like my body is moving through wet cement or something. Walking to the bus feels like it takes so much effort, even if it’s the same walk I’ve always done. And when someone asks me a simple question, even at work, I feel like there’s this delay before I can answer. I know what they said, but getting the words out feels slow, like I have to drag them up.\n\nI’ve noticed people kind of waiting for me to respond, and then I get embarrassed, so I try to cover it by nodding or saying “sorry, one second,” but it still feels like I’m moving and talking slower than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I haven’t been weighing myself every day or anything, so I can’t give you an exact number, but my clothes are definitely fitting different. My work pants especially — they used to fit pretty normal, and now they’re looser around the waist, like I keep having to pull them up or adjust them during my shift.\n\nMy appetite’s been really inconsistent. There are times where I’ll realize it’s late in the day and I haven’t really eaten anything, not because I’m trying not to, just because I forget or nothing sounds good. And when I do make myself eat, food kind of tastes like cardboard sometimes. Like I know I’m chewing something I usually like, but it just feels… pointless, I guess. So yeah, I think I’ve lost some weight, even if I don’t know exactly how much.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things that you think you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. A lot. It’s kind of embarrassing how much, because some of it is small stuff that I know other people probably wouldn’t even remember, but I keep turning it over in my head like it means something terrible about me.\n\nLike at work, if I make any mistake at the register — even something like counting change wrong for a second, or needing to call my manager over, or taking too long with a customer — I’ll replay it later over and over. I’ll be lying there thinking, why did I do that, why can’t I just be normal and do my job right, everyone must think I’m useless. And it doesn’t just feel like, “Oh, I made a mistake.” It feels like proof that I’m no good, like I’m letting my manager down and making things harder for everyone.\n\nAnd then it spreads to my family too. I keep thinking I’m disappointing them, like I’m not showing up the way I should. My little nieces especially… I feel awful because I think I should be more present, more fun, more like myself around them, and instead I feel like I’m just this person who’s failing everybody. Even if no one says that to me, my mind keeps saying it. Like I’ve let everybody down somehow, even when I can’t point to one huge thing I did.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing this heaviness and slowness consistently over the past four months, or has it varied?\n\nPatient: It’s varied, definitely. It hasn’t been the same the whole time. Earlier on, I was almost too sped up in my body, if that makes sense. Like at work I couldn’t just stand at the register and wait — I’d be pacing around the store, straightening things that were already straight, moving candy around, fixing shelves, wiping the counter again. It felt like I had this pressure in me and if I stopped moving I’d feel worse.\n\nBut lately it’s been more the opposite. The heaviness and slowness is more recent, or at least it’s what I notice most now. Even simple stuff feels like it takes effort, like walking to the bus feels way harder than it should, and when someone asks me a question, I know what they’re saying, but my answer comes out slow. Like there’s a pause before I can get the words moving.\n\nSo no, not consistently the same for the whole four months. It kind of shifted from restless and pacing to this slowed-down, heavy feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, such as hearing voices or seeing things that others can't?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve had that happen, and I don’t really like saying it out loud because it sounds, I don’t know, it sounds like I’m making it up or being dramatic, but it felt real when it was happening.\n\nIt’s mostly been at work, at the register. I’ll be standing there scanning things or waiting for someone’s card to go through, and I’ll hear what sounds like people right behind me whispering. Like close enough that I think a customer is standing too close, or someone is trying to get my attention without fully talking to me. A couple times it sounded like my name, like someone whispered it just behind my shoulder, and I’d turn around expecting somebody to be there, but there wouldn’t be anyone.\n\nAnd sometimes it wasn’t just my name. It would be like… little comments, like I was messing everything up, or doing it wrong, or that people were noticing. Not always loud, more like whispering, like customers talking under their breath but aimed at me. And I’d look around and everyone would just be doing normal stuff, or there wouldn’t even be anyone close enough to have said it.\n\nI kept trying to tell myself maybe it was the store noise, or the speakers, or someone in another aisle, because it’s embarrassing and scary to think it’s not that. But there were times where it really felt like someone was right behind me saying it, and then I’d turn and nobody was there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any thoughts that you think are being inserted into your mind or that you're being controlled by an outside force?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that exact way. Like, I’ve had thoughts that feel intrusive or like they come out of nowhere, especially when I’m already stressed, but I don’t know that I’ve felt like someone else was putting them there.\n\nAnd I don’t think I’ve felt controlled by something outside me, like someone moving me around or making me do things. I mean, sometimes I feel overwhelmed by my own mind, like I can’t get it to quiet down, but that still feels like it’s coming from me, if that makes sense. Not like a person or a force is controlling me.\n\nI’m not totally sure what would count, though. Like if you mean literally feeling like my thoughts aren’t mine, then no, I don’t think I’ve noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past week where you felt somewhat better, or has the heaviness and sadness been constant?\n\nPatient: It’s been pretty constant this past week, honestly. I mean, there might be tiny moments where I’m distracted or I’m not actively thinking about how bad I feel, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had a day where I felt better in any real way.\n\nThe tiredness is the biggest thing. That’s really why I came in, because I’m so tired I can barely get through a shift. It’s not like normal tired where you sleep or sit down for a while and then you get some energy back. It feels like my whole body is filled with wet sand, like every movement takes more effort than it should. Even when I rest, I don’t feel restored. I can lie down, or sit on the couch doing nothing, and when I get back up it’s like I never rested at all.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve had a good day this week. Maybe a few better minutes here and there, but the heaviness has mostly just stayed there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been able to enjoy anything at all recently, even if it's just for a moment?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I keep trying to, because I know I’m supposed to have things that make me feel like myself, but they just don’t land the same.\n\nLike cooking, for example. I usually love cooking for myself. Even if it’s something simple, I used to like picking what I wanted, chopping things, making it taste right, feeling like I did one nice thing for myself. Lately it just feels like a chore, like I’m moving food around because I have to eat, not because I care.\n\nAnd guitar too… I’ll pick it up sometimes because I think maybe it’ll help, and then I just sit there holding it. I might strum a little, but it feels flat, like there’s no feeling behind it. Same with hiking. I used to actually look forward to being outside and getting away from everything, but now even the thought of getting dressed and going somewhere feels pointless.\n\nEven going to my family’s place, which usually helps at least a little, feels like I’m kind of pretending. I’ll sit there and try to act normal and respond the right way, but inside I’m not really enjoying it. It’s like I remember that I’m supposed to care about these things, but I can’t actually feel it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you lost touch with reality?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think maybe I have, or at least I’ve had moments where I couldn’t trust what was happening around me.\n\nThe main thing was at work, at the register. I’d be ringing people up and I would hear what sounded like customers standing right behind me, whispering my name. Or saying things like I was messing everything up. Not loud like someone yelling, more like that low talking people do when they don’t want you to hear them but you still can. And it felt so real in the moment that I’d turn around expecting someone to be there.\n\nBut then nobody would be there. Or the people nearby would just be shopping or looking at their phones, not even paying attention to me. And then I’d feel embarrassed, because I’m standing there looking around like I heard something, but there’s nothing there.\n\nIt happened more than once, and it really scared me, because it wasn’t just me worrying someone was judging me. It sounded like an actual voice right behind me. I don’t know if that counts as losing touch with reality, but it definitely felt like my brain was telling me something was happening that wasn’t actually happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any visual hallucinations, such as seeing things that others cannot?\n\nPatient: Seeing things… not really, not like full-on seeing a person or something standing there that nobody else can see.\n\nFor me it’s been more hearing things. Like at the register, I would be working and I’d suddenly hear what sounded like customers right behind me whispering my name, or saying I was messing everything up. It was close enough and clear enough that I’d turn around because I really thought someone was there talking about me. But then there wouldn’t be anyone there, or nobody would be looking at me or acting like they said anything.\n\nSo visually, I don’t think so. It’s more that I hear it and then I look because I expect to see someone, and then there’s nothing there.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt no sadness or depression at all?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not a whole day, at least.\n\nIt’s more like this low, empty feeling has just been there all the time. Maybe it shifts a little, like some hours are less heavy than others, but I can’t think of a day in the past two weeks where I woke up and felt actually okay, like no sadness or depression at all.\n\nAnd honestly, it’s been going on longer than just two weeks. That empty part has been around for about four months now. At first there was this rush of energy at work, like I was pushing through everything and moving fast, but after that passed, the low feeling stayed. It didn’t really lift. It’s like everything settled into this dull, heavy place, and I’ve just been living inside it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Yeah… most of the day, pretty much every day. It’s not like it only comes at night or only when something bad happens. It’s more like I wake up and it’s already there, that low, empty feeling, and then it just kind of follows me through the day.

It’s been about four months now, I think. I keep thinking back to when I first noticed it, and there was this period before where I had all this energy at work, like I was pushing myself really hard and moving fast, almost running on something. But after that passed, I didn’t just go back to normal. I kind of dropped into this flat, heavy place, and it stayed.

Some parts of the day are maybe a little less intense than others, like if I’m distracted or busy, but it doesn’t really go away. It’s still underneath everything. So yes, I’d say for most of each day, for months now, it’s been there.

I keep trying to look back and figure out exactly when it started, because at first I thought maybe I was just coming down from this period where I had a lot of energy at work, like I was pushing really hard and running on adrenaline or something.\n\nBut that passed, and then this low, empty feeling just… stayed. It didn’t lift the way I expected it to. So yeah, around four months of feeling like this, where everything feels heavy and flat and I’m not really myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite, feeling restless and fidgety?\n\nPatient: Yeah… both, actually, but at different times. At first I was almost the opposite of slowed down. I couldn’t stop moving. Like at the store, I’d be pacing around even when there wasn’t really anything urgent to do, straightening the candy shelves, fixing the bags, lining up receipts, wiping the counter again even if I’d already done it. It wasn’t even productive, exactly. It was more like if I stood still, I felt like I was going to crawl out of my skin.\n\nBut lately it’s changed. Now I feel… heavy. Like my body is moving through wet cement or something. Walking to the bus feels like it takes so much effort, even if it’s the same walk I’ve always done. I know what they said, but getting the words out feels slow, like I have to drag them up.\n\nI’ve noticed people kind of waiting for me to respond, and then I get embarrassed, so I try to cover it by nodding or saying “sorry, one second,” but it still feels like I’m moving and talking slower than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I haven’t been weighing myself every day or anything, so I can’t give you an exact number, but my clothes are definitely fitting different. My work pants especially — they used to fit pretty normal, and now they’re looser around the waist, like I keep having to pull them up or adjust them during my shift.\n\nMy appetite’s been really inconsistent. There are times where I’ll realize it’s late in the day and I haven’t really eaten anything, not because I’m trying not to, just because I forget or nothing sounds good. And when I do make myself eat, food kind of tastes like cardboard sometimes. Like I know I’m chewing something I usually like, but it just feels… pointless, I guess. So yeah, I think I’ve lost some weight, even if I don’t know exactly how much.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things that you think you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. A lot. It’s kind of embarrassing how much, because some of it is small stuff that I know other people probably wouldn’t even remember, but I keep turning it over in my head like it means something terrible about me.\n\nLike at work, if I make any mistake at the register — even something like counting change wrong for a second, or needing to call my manager over, or taking too long with a customer — I’ll replay it later over and over. I’ll be lying there thinking, why did I do that, why can’t I just be normal and do my job right, everyone must think I’m useless. And it doesn’t just feel like, “Oh, I made a mistake.” It feels like proof that I’m no good, like I’m letting my manager down and making things harder for everyone.\n\nAnd then it spreads to my family too. I keep thinking I’m disappointing them, like I’m not showing up the way I should. My little nieces especially… I feel awful because I think I should be more present, more fun, more like myself around them, and instead I feel like I’m just this person who’s failing everybody. It hasn’t been the same the whole time. Earlier on, I was almost too sped up in my body, if that makes sense. Like at work I couldn’t just stand at the register and wait — I’d be pacing around the store, straightening things that were already straight, moving candy around, fixing shelves, wiping the counter again. It felt like I had this pressure in me and if I stopped moving I’d feel worse.\n\nBut lately it’s been more the opposite. The heaviness and slowness is more recent, or at least it’s what I notice most now. Like there’s a pause before I can get the words moving.\n\nSo no, not consistently the same for the whole four months. It kind of shifted from restless and pacing to this slowed-down, heavy feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, such as hearing voices or seeing things that others can't?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve had that happen, and I don’t really like saying it out loud because it sounds, I don’t know, it sounds like I’m making it up or being dramatic, but it felt real when it was happening.\n\nIt’s mostly been at work, at the register. I’ll be standing there scanning things or waiting for someone’s card to go through, and I’ll hear what sounds like people right behind me whispering. Like close enough that I think a customer is standing too close, or someone is trying to get my attention without fully talking to me. A couple times it sounded like my name, like someone whispered it just behind my shoulder, and I’d turn around expecting somebody to be there, but there wouldn’t be anyone.\n\nAnd sometimes it wasn’t just my name. It would be like… little comments, like I was messing everything up, or doing it wrong, or that people were noticing. Not always loud, more like whispering, like customers talking under their breath but aimed at me. And I’d look around and everyone would just be doing normal stuff, or there wouldn’t even be anyone close enough to have said it.\n\nI kept trying to tell myself maybe it was the store noise, or the speakers, or someone in another aisle, because it’s embarrassing and scary to think it’s not that. But there were times where it really felt like someone was right behind me saying it, and then I’d turn and nobody was there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any thoughts that you think are being inserted into your mind or that you're being controlled by an outside force?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that exact way. Like, I’ve had thoughts that feel intrusive or like they come out of nowhere, especially when I’m already stressed, but I don’t know that I’ve felt like someone else was putting them there.\n\nAnd I don’t think I’ve felt controlled by something outside me, like someone moving me around or making me do things. Like at the register, I would be working and I’d suddenly hear what sounded like customers right behind me whispering my name, or saying I was messing everything up. It was close enough and clear enough that I’d turn around because I really thought someone was there talking about me. But then there wouldn’t be anyone there, or nobody would be looking at me or acting like they said anything.\n\nSo visually, I don’t think so. It’s more that I hear it and then I look because I expect to see someone, and then there’s nothing there.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt no sadness or depression at all?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not a whole day, at least.\n\nIt’s more like this low, empty feeling has just been there all the time. Maybe it shifts a little, like some hours are less heavy than others, but I can’t think of a day in the past two weeks where I woke up and felt actually okay, like no sadness or depression at all.\n\nAnd honestly, it’s been going on longer than just two weeks. That empty part has been around for about four months now. At first there was this rush of energy at work, like I was pushing through everything and moving fast, but after that passed, the low feeling stayed. It didn’t really lift. It’s like everything settled into this dull, heavy place, and I’ve just been living inside it.\n\nDoctor: Have your feelings of sadness or depression been present for most of each day over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the day, pretty much every day. I’m not sure… I don’t think so, not in the way that sounds, like something else was literally moving my body or putting thoughts into my head.

So yeah, around four months of feeling like this, where everything feels heavy and flat and I’m not really myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite, feeling restless and fidgety?\n\nPatient: Yeah… both, actually, but at different times. At first I was almost the opposite of slowed down. I couldn’t stop moving. Like at the store, I’d be pacing around even when there wasn’t really anything urgent to do, straightening the candy shelves, fixing the bags, lining up receipts, wiping the counter again even if I’d already done it. It wasn’t even productive, exactly. It was more like if I stood still, I felt like I was going to crawl out of my skin.\n\nBut lately it’s changed. Now I feel… heavy. Like my body is moving through wet cement or something. Walking to the bus feels like it takes so much effort, even if it’s the same walk I’ve always done. And when someone asks me a simple question, even at work, I feel like there’s this delay before I can answer. I know what they said, but getting the words out feels slow, like I have to drag them up.\n\nI’ve noticed people kind of waiting for me to respond, and then I get embarrassed, so I try to cover it by nodding or saying “sorry, one second,” but it still feels like I’m moving and talking slower than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I haven’t been weighing myself every day or anything, so I can’t give you an exact number, but my clothes are definitely fitting different. My work pants especially — they used to fit pretty normal, and now they’re looser around the waist, like I keep having to pull them up or adjust them during my shift.\n\nMy appetite’s been really inconsistent. There are times where I’ll realize it’s late in the day and I haven’t really eaten anything, not because I’m trying not to, just because I forget or nothing sounds good. And when I do make myself eat, food kind of tastes like cardboard sometimes. Like I know I’m chewing something I usually like, but it just feels… pointless, I guess. So yeah, I think I’ve lost some weight, even if I don’t know exactly how much.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things that you think you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. A lot. It’s kind of embarrassing how much, because some of it is small stuff that I know other people probably wouldn’t even remember, but I keep turning it over in my head like it means something terrible about me.\n\nLike at work, if I make any mistake at the register — even something like counting change wrong for a second, or needing to call my manager over, or taking too long with a customer — I’ll replay it later over and over. I’ll be lying there thinking, why did I do that, why can’t I just be normal and do my job right, everyone must think I’m useless. And it doesn’t just feel like, “Oh, I made a mistake.” It feels like proof that I’m no good, like I’m letting my manager down and making things harder for everyone.\n\nAnd then it spreads to my family too. I keep thinking I’m disappointing them, like I’m not showing up the way I should. My little nieces especially… I feel awful because I think I should be more present, more fun, more like myself around them, and instead I feel like I’m just this person who’s failing everybody. Even if no one says that to me, my mind keeps saying it. Like I’ve let everybody down somehow, even when I can’t point to one huge thing I did.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing this heaviness and slowness consistently over the past four months, or has it varied?\n\nPatient: It’s varied, definitely. It hasn’t been the same the whole time. Earlier on, I was almost too sped up in my body, if that makes sense. Like at work I couldn’t just stand at the register and wait — I’d be pacing around the store, straightening things that were already straight, moving candy around, fixing shelves, wiping the counter again. It felt like I had this pressure in me and if I stopped moving I’d feel worse.\n\nBut lately it’s been more the opposite. The heaviness and slowness is more recent, or at least it’s what I notice most now. Even simple stuff feels like it takes effort, like walking to the bus feels way harder than it should, and when someone asks me a question, I know what they’re saying, but my answer comes out slow. Like there’s a pause before I can get the words moving.\n\nSo no, not consistently the same for the whole four months. It kind of shifted from restless and pacing to this slowed-down, heavy feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, such as hearing voices or seeing things that others can't?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve had that happen, and I don’t really like saying it out loud because it sounds, I don’t know, it sounds like I’m making it up or being dramatic, but it felt real when it was happening.\n\nIt’s mostly been at work, at the register. I’ll be standing there scanning things or waiting for someone’s card to go through, and I’ll hear what sounds like people right behind me whispering. Like close enough that I think a customer is standing too close, or someone is trying to get my attention without fully talking to me. A couple times it sounded like my name, like someone whispered it just behind my shoulder, and I’d turn around expecting somebody to be there, but there wouldn’t be anyone.\n\nAnd sometimes it wasn’t just my name. It would be like… little comments, like I was messing everything up, or doing it wrong, or that people were noticing. Not always loud, more like whispering, like customers talking under their breath but aimed at me. And I’d look around and everyone would just be doing normal stuff, or there wouldn’t even be anyone close enough to have said it.\n\nI kept trying to tell myself maybe it was the store noise, or the speakers, or someone in another aisle, because it’s embarrassing and scary to think it’s not that. But there were times where it really felt like someone was right behind me saying it, and then I’d turn and nobody was there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any thoughts that you think are being inserted into your mind or that you're being controlled by an outside force?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that exact way. Like, I’ve had thoughts that feel intrusive or like they come out of nowhere, especially when I’m already stressed, but I don’t know that I’ve felt like someone else was putting them there.\n\nAnd I don’t think I’ve felt controlled by something outside me, like someone moving me around or making me do things. I mean, sometimes I feel overwhelmed by my own mind, like I can’t get it to quiet down, but that still feels like it’s coming from me, if that makes sense. Not like a person or a force is controlling me.\n\nI’m not totally sure what would count, though. Like if you mean literally feeling like my thoughts aren’t mine, then no, I don’t think I’ve noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past week where you felt somewhat better, or has the heaviness and sadness been constant?\n\nPatient: It’s been pretty constant this past week, honestly. I mean, there might be tiny moments where I’m distracted or I’m not actively thinking about how bad I feel, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had a day where I felt better in any real way.\n\nThe tiredness is the biggest thing. That’s really why I came in, because I’m so tired I can barely get through a shift. It’s not like normal tired where you sleep or sit down for a while and then you get some energy back. It feels like my whole body is filled with wet sand, like every movement takes more effort than it should. Even when I rest, I don’t feel restored. I can lie down, or sit on the couch doing nothing, and when I get back up it’s like I never rested at all.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve had a good day this week. Maybe a few better minutes here and there, but the heaviness has mostly just stayed there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been able to enjoy anything at all recently, even if it's just for a moment?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I keep trying to, because I know I’m supposed to have things that make me feel like myself, but they just don’t land the same.\n\nLike cooking, for example. I usually love cooking for myself. Even if it’s something simple, I used to like picking what I wanted, chopping things, making it taste right, feeling like I did one nice thing for myself. Lately it just feels like a chore, like I’m moving food around because I have to eat, not because I care.\n\nAnd guitar too… I’ll pick it up sometimes because I think maybe it’ll help, and then I just sit there holding it. I might strum a little, but it feels flat, like there’s no feeling behind it. Same with hiking. I used to actually look forward to being outside and getting away from everything, but now even the thought of getting dressed and going somewhere feels pointless.\n\nEven going to my family’s place, which usually helps at least a little, feels like I’m kind of pretending. I’ll sit there and try to act normal and respond the right way, but inside I’m not really enjoying it. It’s like I remember that I’m supposed to care about these things, but I can’t actually feel it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you lost touch with reality?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think maybe I have, or at least I’ve had moments where I couldn’t trust what was happening around me.\n\nThe main thing was at work, at the register. I’d be ringing people up and I would hear what sounded like customers standing right behind me, whispering my name. Or saying things like I was messing everything up. Not loud like someone yelling, more like that low talking people do when they don’t want you to hear them but you still can. And it felt so real in the moment that I’d turn around expecting someone to be there.\n\nBut then nobody would be there. Or the people nearby would just be shopping or looking at their phones, not even paying attention to me. And then I’d feel embarrassed, because I’m standing there looking around like I heard something, but there’s nothing there.\n\nIt happened more than once, and it really scared me, because it wasn’t just me worrying someone was judging me. It sounded like an actual voice right behind me. I don’t know if that counts as losing touch with reality, but it definitely felt like my brain was telling me something was happening that wasn’t actually happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any visual hallucinations, such as seeing things that others cannot?\n\nPatient: Seeing things… not really, not like full-on seeing a person or something standing there that nobody else can see.\n\nFor me it’s been more hearing things. Like at the register, I would be working and I’d suddenly hear what sounded like customers right behind me whispering my name, or saying I was messing everything up. It was close enough and clear enough that I’d turn around because I really thought someone was there talking about me. But then there wouldn’t be anyone there, or nobody would be looking at me or acting like they said anything.\n\nSo visually, I don’t think so. It’s more that I hear it and then I look because I expect to see someone, and then there’s nothing there.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt no sadness or depression at all?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Maybe it shifts a little, like some hours are less heavy than others, but I can’t think of a day in the past two weeks where I woke up and felt actually okay, like no sadness or depression at all.\n\nAnd honestly, it’s been going on longer than just two weeks. That empty part has been around for about four months now. At first there was this rush of energy at work, like I was pushing through everything and moving fast, but after that passed, the low feeling stayed. It didn’t really lift. It’s like everything settled into this dull, heavy place, and I’ve just been living inside it.\n\nDoctor: Have your feelings of sadness or depression been present for most of each day over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the day, pretty much every day. It’s not like it only comes at night or only when something bad happens. I keep thinking back to when I first noticed it, and there was this period before where I had all this energy at work, like I was pushing myself really hard and moving fast, almost running on something. Yeah… yes, lately I have. It’s kind of strange because at first it was almost the opposite. I remember at work I couldn’t stop moving around the store, like pacing up and down the aisles and straightening things that were already straight, fixing displays, checking the same shelves over and over. I felt like if I stood still too long I’d crawl out of my skin or something.

But lately it’s been more like everything takes extra effort. Even walking to the bus feels like I’m dragging myself through mud. I’ll know I need to get moving, but my body just feels heavy and slow, like there’s a delay between deciding to do something and actually doing it.

And with talking too… if someone asks me a question, even a simple one, I feel like I’m taking too long to answer. I hear the question, but getting the words out feels slow. Sometimes I can tell people are waiting, and that makes me feel embarrassed, because I’m not trying to ignore them or be difficult. It just feels like my brain and my body are both moving slower than they should.

I haven’t been weighing myself every day or anything, so I can’t give you an exact number, but my clothes are definitely fitting different. There are times where I’ll realize it’s late in the day and I haven’t really eaten anything, not because I’m trying not to, just because I forget or nothing sounds good. And when I do make myself eat, food kind of tastes like cardboard sometimes. Like I know I’m chewing something I usually like, but it just feels… pointless, I guess. So yeah, I think I’ve lost some weight, even if I don’t know exactly how much.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things that you think you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. A lot. It’s kind of embarrassing how much, because some of it is small stuff that I know other people probably wouldn’t even remember, but I keep turning it over in my head like it means something terrible about me.\n\nLike at work, if I make any mistake at the register — even something like counting change wrong for a second, or needing to call my manager over, or taking too long with a customer — I’ll replay it later over and over. I’ll be lying there thinking, why did I do that, why can’t I just be normal and do my job right, everyone must think I’m useless. And it doesn’t just feel like, “Oh, I made a mistake.” It feels like proof that I’m no good, like I’m letting my manager down and making things harder for everyone.\n\nAnd then it spreads to my family too. I keep thinking I’m disappointing them, like I’m not showing up the way I should. My little nieces especially… I feel awful because I think I should be more present, more fun, more like myself around them, and instead I feel like I’m just this person who’s failing everybody. Even if no one says that to me, my mind keeps saying it. Like I’ve let everybody down somehow, even when I can’t point to one huge thing I did.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing this heaviness and slowness consistently over the past four months, or has it varied?\n\nPatient: It’s varied, definitely. It hasn’t been the same the whole time. Earlier on, I was almost too sped up in my body, if that makes sense. Like at work I couldn’t just stand at the register and wait — I’d be pacing around the store, straightening things that were already straight, moving candy around, fixing shelves, wiping the counter again. I’ll be standing there scanning things or waiting for someone’s card to go through, and I’ll hear what sounds like people right behind me whispering. Like, I’ve had thoughts that feel intrusive or like they come out of nowhere, especially when I’m already stressed, but I don’t know that I’ve felt like someone else was putting them there.\n\nAnd I don’t think I’ve felt controlled by something outside me, like someone moving me around or making me do things. I mean, sometimes I feel overwhelmed by my own mind, like I can’t get it to quiet down, but that still feels like it’s coming from me, if that makes sense. I mean, there might be tiny moments where I’m distracted or I’m not actively thinking about how bad I feel, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had a day where I felt better in any real way.\n\nThe tiredness is the biggest thing. That’s really why I came in, because I’m so tired I can barely get through a shift. It’s not like normal tired where you sleep or sit down for a while and then you get some energy back. It feels like my whole body is filled with wet sand, like every movement takes more effort than it should. Even when I rest, I don’t feel restored. I can lie down, or sit on the couch doing nothing, and when I get back up it’s like I never rested at all.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve had a good day this week. Maybe a few better minutes here and there, but the heaviness has mostly just stayed there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been able to enjoy anything at all recently, even if it's just for a moment?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I keep trying to, because I know I’m supposed to have things that make me feel like myself, but they just don’t land the same.\n\nLike cooking, for example. I usually love cooking for myself. Even if it’s something simple, I used to like picking what I wanted, chopping things, making it taste right, feeling like I did one nice thing for myself. Lately it just feels like a chore, like I’m moving food around because I have to eat, not because I care.\n\nAnd guitar too… I’ll pick it up sometimes because I think maybe it’ll help, and then I just sit there holding it. I might strum a little, but it feels flat, like there’s no feeling behind it. Same with hiking. I used to actually look forward to being outside and getting away from everything, but now even the thought of getting dressed and going somewhere feels pointless.\n\nEven going to my family’s place, which usually helps at least a little, feels like I’m kind of pretending. I’ll sit there and try to act normal and respond the right way, but inside I’m not really enjoying it. It’s like I remember that I’m supposed to care about these things, but I can’t actually feel it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you lost touch with reality?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think maybe I have, or at least I’ve had moments where I couldn’t trust what was happening around me.\n\nThe main thing was at work, at the register. I’d be ringing people up and I would hear what sounded like customers standing right behind me, whispering my name. Or saying things like I was messing everything up. Not loud like someone yelling, more like that low talking people do when they don’t want you to hear them but you still can. And it felt so real in the moment that I’d turn around expecting someone to be there.\n\nBut then nobody would be there. Or the people nearby would just be shopping or looking at their phones, not even paying attention to me. And then I’d feel embarrassed, because I’m standing there looking around like I heard something, but there’s nothing there.\n\nIt happened more than once, and it really scared me, because it wasn’t just me worrying someone was judging me. It sounded like an actual voice right behind me. I don’t know if that counts as losing touch with reality, but it definitely felt like my brain was telling me something was happening that wasn’t actually happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any visual hallucinations, such as seeing things that others cannot?\n\nPatient: Seeing things… not really, not like full-on seeing a person or something standing there that nobody else can see.\n\nFor me it’s been more hearing things. Like at the register, I would be working and I’d suddenly hear what sounded like customers right behind me whispering my name, or saying I was messing everything up. It was close enough and clear enough that I’d turn around because I really thought someone was there talking about me. But then there wouldn’t be anyone there, or nobody would be looking at me or acting like they said anything.\n\nSo visually, I don’t think so. It’s more that I hear it and then I look because I expect to see someone, and then there’s nothing there.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt no sadness or depression at all?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not a whole day, at least.\n\nIt’s more like this low, empty feeling has just been there all the time. Maybe it shifts a little, like some hours are less heavy than others, but I can’t think of a day in the past two weeks where I woke up and felt actually okay, like no sadness or depression at all.\n\nAnd honestly, it’s been going on longer than just two weeks. That empty part has been around for about four months now. At first there was this rush of energy at work, like I was pushing through everything and moving fast, but after that passed, the low feeling stayed. It didn’t really lift. It’s like everything settled into this dull, heavy place, and I’ve just been living inside it.\n\nDoctor: Have your feelings of sadness or depression been present for most of each day over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the day, pretty much every day. It’s not like it only comes at night or only when something bad happens. It’s more like I wake up and it’s already there, that low, empty feeling, and then it just kind of follows me through the day.\n\nIt’s been about four months now, I think. I keep thinking back to when I first noticed it, and there was this period before where I had all this energy at work, like I was pushing myself really hard and moving fast, almost running on something. But after that passed, I didn’t just go back to normal. I kind of dropped into this flat, heavy place, and it stayed.\n\nSome parts of the day are maybe a little less intense than others, like if I’m distracted or busy, but it doesn’t really go away. It’s still underneath everything. So yes, I’d say for most of each day, for months now, it’s been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt like your thoughts or actions were being controlled by something outside of yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think so, not in the way that sounds, like something else was literally moving my body or putting thoughts into my head.\n\nI’ve had times where I didn’t feel totally like myself, or where I second-guessed why I was doing something, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I don’t remember feeling like some outside force was controlling my actions, like making me say things or move my hands or anything like that.\n\nWith thoughts… I mean, sometimes my mind feels really loud or hard to slow down, but I wouldn’t say it feels controlled from outside me. It feels like it’s still me, just… not always easy to manage. Like, I’ve had thoughts that feel intrusive or like they come out of nowhere, especially when I’m already stressed, but I don’t know that I’ve felt like someone else was putting them there.\n\nAnd I don’t think I’ve felt controlled by something outside me, like someone moving me around or making me do things. I mean, sometimes I feel overwhelmed by my own mind, like I can’t get it to quiet down, but that still feels like it’s coming from me, if that makes sense. Not like a person or a force is controlling me.\n\nI’m not totally sure what would count, though. Like if you mean literally feeling like my thoughts aren’t mine, then no, I don’t think I’ve noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past week where you felt somewhat better, or has the heaviness and sadness been constant?\n\nPatient: It’s been pretty constant this past week, honestly. I mean, there might be tiny moments where I’m distracted or I’m not actively thinking about how bad I feel, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had a day where I felt better in any real way.\n\nThe tiredness is the biggest thing. That’s really why I came in, because I’m so tired I can barely get through a shift. It’s not like normal tired where you sleep or sit down for a while and then you get some energy back. It feels like my whole body is filled with wet sand, like every movement takes more effort than it should. Even when I rest, I don’t feel restored. I can lie down, or sit on the couch doing nothing, and when I get back up it’s like I never rested at all.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve had a good day this week. Maybe a few better minutes here and there, but the heaviness has mostly just stayed there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been able to enjoy anything at all recently, even if it's just for a moment?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I keep trying to, because I know I’m supposed to have things that make me feel like myself, but they just don’t land the same.\n\nLike cooking, for example. I usually love cooking for myself. Even if it’s something simple, I used to like picking what I wanted, chopping things, making it taste right, feeling like I did one nice thing for myself. Lately it just feels like a chore, like I’m moving food around because I have to eat, not because I care.\n\nAnd guitar too… I’ll pick it up sometimes because I think maybe it’ll help, and then I just sit there holding it. I might strum a little, but it feels flat, like there’s no feeling behind it. Same with hiking. I used to actually look forward to being outside and getting away from everything, but now even the thought of getting dressed and going somewhere feels pointless.\n\nEven going to my family’s place, which usually helps at least a little, feels like I’m kind of pretending. I’ll sit there and try to act normal and respond the right way, but inside I’m not really enjoying it. It’s like I remember that I’m supposed to care about these things, but I can’t actually feel it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you lost touch with reality?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think maybe I have, or at least I’ve had moments where I couldn’t trust what was happening around me.\n\nThe main thing was at work, at the register. I’d be ringing people up and I would hear what sounded like customers standing right behind me, whispering my name. Or saying things like I was messing everything up. Not loud like someone yelling, more like that low talking people do when they don’t want you to hear them but you still can. And it felt so real in the moment that I’d turn around expecting someone to be there.\n\nBut then nobody would be there. Or the people nearby would just be shopping or looking at their phones, not even paying attention to me. And then I’d feel embarrassed, because I’m standing there looking around like I heard something, but there’s nothing there.\n\nIt happened more than once, and it really scared me, because it wasn’t just me worrying someone was judging me. It sounded like an actual voice right behind me. I don’t know if that counts as losing touch with reality, but it definitely felt like my brain was telling me something was happening that wasn’t actually happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any visual hallucinations, such as seeing things that others cannot?\n\nPatient: Seeing things… not really, not like full-on seeing a person or something standing there that nobody else can see.\n\nFor me it’s been more hearing things. Like at the register, I would be working and I’d suddenly hear what sounded like customers right behind me whispering my name, or saying I was messing everything up. It was close enough and clear enough that I’d turn around because I really thought someone was there talking about me. But then there wouldn’t be anyone there, or nobody would be looking at me or acting like they said anything.\n\nSo visually, I don’t think so. It’s more that I hear it and then I look because I expect to see someone, and then there’s nothing there.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt no sadness or depression at all?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not a whole day, at least.\n\nIt’s more like this low, empty feeling has just been there all the time. Maybe it shifts a little, like some hours are less heavy than others, but I can’t think of a day in the past two weeks where I woke up and felt actually okay, like no sadness or depression at all.\n\nAnd honestly, it’s been going on longer than just two weeks. That empty part has been around for about four months now. At first there was this rush of energy at work, like I was pushing through everything and moving fast, but after that passed, the low feeling stayed. It didn’t really lift. It’s like everything settled into this dull, heavy place, and I’ve just been living inside it.\n\nDoctor: Have your feelings of sadness or depression been present for most of each day over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the day, pretty much every day. It’s not like it only comes at night or only when something bad happens. It’s more like I wake up and it’s already there, that low, empty feeling, and then it just kind of follows me through the day.\n\nIt’s been about four months now, I think. I keep thinking back to when I first noticed it, and there was this period before where I had all this energy at work, like I was pushing myself really hard and moving fast, almost running on something. But after that passed, I didn’t just go back to normal. I kind of dropped into this flat, heavy place, and it stayed.\n\nSome parts of the day are maybe a little less intense than others, like if I’m distracted or busy, but it doesn’t really go away. It’s still underneath everything. So yes, I’d say for most of each day, for months now, it’s been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt like your thoughts or actions were being controlled by something outside of yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think so, not in the way that sounds, like something else was literally moving my body or putting thoughts into my head.\n\nI’ve had times where I didn’t feel totally like myself, or where I second-guessed why I was doing something, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I don’t remember feeling like some outside force was controlling my actions, like making me say things or move my hands or anything like that.\n\nWith thoughts… I mean, sometimes my mind feels really loud or hard to slow down, but I wouldn’t say it feels controlled from outside me. It feels like it’s still me, just… not always easy to manage. So I guess my answer is no, or at least I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you recently experienced any periods where you felt like you were moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, lately I have. It’s kind of strange because at first it was almost the opposite. I remember at work I couldn’t stop moving around the store, like pacing up and down the aisles and straightening things that were already straight, fixing displays, checking the same shelves over and over. I felt like if I stood still too long I’d crawl out of my skin or something.\n\nBut lately it’s been more like everything takes extra effort. Even walking to the bus feels like I’m dragging myself through mud. I’ll know I need to get moving, but my body just feels heavy and slow, like there’s a delay between deciding to do something and actually doing it.\n\nAnd with talking too… if someone asks me a question, even a simple one, I feel like I’m taking too long to answer. I hear the question, but getting the words out feels slow. Sometimes I can tell people are waiting, and that makes me feel embarrassed, because I’m not trying to ignore them or be difficult. It just feels like my brain and my body are both moving slower than they should.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. No… not more than two years. It feels like it’s been forever, honestly, because every day has been so heavy, but when I really count it out, it’s been about four months.
